### Freshening

Define:
```
Fixpoint max_list (l : list nat) : nat :=
  match l with [] => 0 | x::xs => Nat.max x (max_list xs) end.
Definition fresh (l : list atom) : atom := S (max_list l).
Lemma fresh_not_in : forall l, ~ In (fresh l) l.
```
We also need `fresh (l1 ++ l2) ∉ l1` and `∉ l2`. From `fresh_not_in (l1++l2)` and `In_app` inversion. We can just use `fresh (L ++ extra)` and prove `~ In X L` and `~ In X extra` via the not-in-app lemma.

Actually, `max_list` over a list of nats: need `forall x, In x l -> x <= max_list l`, then fresh = S max > all. Standard.

We also need `fv_ty` of all types in a context: `flat_map (fun p => fv_ty (snd p)) Γ`, and for the type context Δ? Δ is a list of atoms (type variables), not types. The environment condition types are from Γ. Also fv_tm_ty of the term. Fine.

### Type substitution lemmas

`ty_subst_env : (atom -> ty) -> ty -> ty`:
```
Fixpoint ty_subst_env σ T :=
  match T with
  | Ty_BVar i => Ty_BVar i
  | Ty_FVar X => σ X
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => Ty_Arrow (ty_subst_env σ T1) (ty_subst_env σ T2)
  | Ty_All T => Ty_All (ty_subst_env σ T)
  end.
```
✓ (substitutes fvars everywhere, including under binders; bvars untouched).

`tm_ty_subst_env σ t`:
```
Fixpoint tm_ty_subst_env σ t :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => tm_fvar x
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs (ty_subst_env σ T) (tm_ty_subst_env σ t1)
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (tm_ty_subst_env σ t1) (tm_ty_subst_env σ t2)
  | tm_tabs t1 => tm_tabs (tm_ty_subst_env σ t1)
  | tm_tapp t1 T => tm_tapp (tm_ty_subst_env σ t1) (ty_subst_env σ T)
  | tm_choice t1 t2 => tm_choice (tm_ty_subst_env σ t1) (tm_ty_subst_env σ t2)
  end.
```
✓.

`tm_subst_env γ t`:
```
Fixpoint tm_subst_env γ t :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => γ x
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (tm_subst_env γ t1)
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (tm_subst_env γ t1) (tm_subst_env γ t2)
  | tm_tabs t1 => tm_tabs (tm_subst_env γ t1)
  | tm_tapp t1 T => tm_tapp (tm_subst_env γ t1) T
  | tm_choice t1 t2 => tm_choice (tm_subst_env γ t1) (tm_subst_env γ t2)
  end.
```
✓.

Note: with this order (`tm_subst_env γ (tm_ty_subst_env σ t)`), the σ substitution goes under tm_abs into the type annotation, but the *type variable* substitution doesn't affect the annotation's term structure. Good.

Commutation lemmas:
1. `tm_ty_subst_env_open_tm : tm_ty_subst_env σ (open_tm_rec k u t) = open_tm_rec k (tm_ty_subst_env σ u) (tm_ty_subst_env σ t)`.
2. `tm_subst_env_open_tm_rec : (forall y, locally_closed_tm (γ y)) -> tm_subst_env γ (open_tm_rec k (tm_fvar x) t) = open_tm_rec k (γ x) (tm_subst_env γ t)`. Hmm, actually we can generalize u = tm_fvar x. In the T_Abs case, u = tm_fvar x. Let's state with fvar x. Maybe we also need it with u = value? No: in the T_Abs case, the opening in the typing premise uses fvar x, and the step uses the argument a. The commutation needs LHS open with fvar x and RHS open with γ x = a. ✓. So state with fvar x.
3. `tm_ty_subst_env_open_tm_ty : tm_ty_subst_env σ (open_tm_ty_rec k U t) = open_tm_ty_rec k (ty_subst_env σ U) (tm_ty_subst_env σ t)`.
4. `tm_subst_env_open_tm_ty : tm_subst_env γ (open_tm_ty_rec k U t) = open_tm_ty_rec k U (tm_subst_env γ t)`.

Let me double check #4: open_tm_ty substitutes type bvars with U in annotations and type args; tm_subst_env substitutes term fvars. They operate on different fields, so they commute. Under tm_tabs, open increments k; both recurse. ✓.

5. `ty_subst_env_open_ty : ty_subst_env σ (open_ty_rec k U T) = open_ty_rec k (ty_subst_env σ U) (ty_subst_env σ T)` — used? Maybe in the lc T_TAbs proof or elsewhere. Let's include.
6. Agreement lemmas using fv lists.

Now, the lc lemmas. Let's write them all:

```
Lemma lc_ty_weaken : forall K K' T, K <= K' -> lc_ty_at K T -> lc_ty_at K' T.
Lemma lc_tm_weaken_K : forall K K' k t, K <= K' -> lc_tm_at K k t -> lc_tm_at K' k t.
Lemma lc_tm_weaken_k : forall K k k' t, k <= k' -> lc_tm_at K k t -> lc_tm_at K k' t.
Lemma lc_tm_weaken : forall K K' k k' t, K <= K' -> k <= k' -> lc_tm_at K k t -> lc_tm_at K' k' t.
```
Proofs by induction.

```
Lemma open_ty_rec_lc_inv : forall K U T, lc_ty_at K (open_ty_rec K U T) -> lc_ty_at (S K) T.
```
Proof induction on T (or on K? K general; the lemma has K as a parameter and the type index changes under All: K→S K; so induction on T with K generalized).
Actually, statement: `forall T K U, lc_ty_at K (open_ty_rec K U T) -> lc_ty_at (S K) T`. Induction T:
- BVar i: open = if i=?K then U else bvar i. Case i=K: need lc_ty_at (S K) (bvar K) → K < S K ✓ by lc_ty_bvar. Case i≠K: open = bvar i; lc gives i<K; need i < S K ✓.
- FVar: lc_ty_fvar.
- Arrow: lc of arrow gives components lc at K; IH each gives at S K; lc_ty_arrow.
- All T': open = Ty_All (open_ty_rec (S K) U T')? Wait open_ty_rec K U (Ty_All T') = Ty_All (open_ty_rec (S K) U T'). lc_ty_at K (Ty_All ...) gives lc_ty_at (S K) (open_ty_rec (S K) U T'). IH with K := S K gives lc_ty_at (S (S K)) T'. Then lc_ty_at (S K) (Ty_All T') needs lc_ty_at (S (S K)) T' ✓.
Good.

```
Lemma open_tm_ty_rec_lc_inv : forall t K k U, lc_tm_at K k (open_tm_ty_rec K U t) -> lc_tm_at (S K) k t.
```
Induction on t, generalizing K (and k):
- bvar i: open_tm_ty leaves it; lc gives i<k; need at S K, k ✓.
- fvar ✓.
- abs T t1: open = tm_abs (open_ty_rec K U T) (open_tm_ty_rec K U t1) [since open_tm_ty_rec k U (tm_abs T t1) = tm_abs (open_ty_rec k U T) (open_tm_ty_rec k U t1); here k=K]. lc_tm_at K k gives lc_ty_at K (open_ty_rec K U T) and lc_tm_at K (S k) (open_tm_ty_rec K U t1). Type lemma: need lc_ty_at (S K) T from `lc_ty_at K (open_ty_rec K U T)` — this is exactly open_ty_rec_lc_inv ✓. IH on t1 with (S k) gives lc_tm_at (S K) (S k) t1. Then lc_tm_abs ✓.
- app/choice ✓.
- tabs t1: open = tm_tabs (open_tm_ty_rec (S K) U t1); lc_tm_at K k gives lc_tm_at (S K) k (open_tm_ty_rec (S K) U t1); IH with K := S K gives lc_tm_at (S (S K)) k t1; then lc_tm_tabs gives lc_tm_at (S K) k (tm_tabs t1) ✓.
- tapp t1 T: open = tm_tapp (open_tm_ty_rec K U t1) (open_ty_rec K U T); lc gives lc_tm_at K k (...) and lc_ty_at K (open_ty_rec K U T). IH gives lc_tm_at (S K) k t1; type inv gives lc_ty_at (S K) T; lc_tm_tapp ✓.

```
Lemma open_tm_rec_lc_inv : forall t K k k' u, lc_tm_at K k (open_tm_rec k' u t) -> k' < k -> lc_tm_at K k t.
```
Induction on t, generalizing k' (and K,k):
- bvar i: open = if i=?k' then u else bvar i. If i=k': open = u; lc_tm_at K k u doesn't give i<k, but k'<k and i=k' so i<k ✓ (use lc_tm_bvar with the hypothesis). If i≠k': open = bvar i; lc gives i<k ✓.
- fvar ✓.
- abs T t1: open = tm_abs T (open_tm_rec (S k') u t1); lc_tm_at K k gives lc_ty_at K T and lc_tm_at K (S k) (open_tm_rec (S k') u t1). IH with k' := S k', k := S k gives lc_tm_at K (S k) t1 (need S k' < S k from k'<k ✓). lc_tm_abs ✓.
- tabs t1: open_tm_rec k' u (tm_tabs t1) = tm_tabs (open_tm_rec k' u t1); lc gives lc_tm_at (S K) k (...); IH with K := S K ✓; lc_tm_tabs.
- app/choice: ✓.
- tapp: open_tm_rec k' u (tm_tapp t1 T) = tm_tapp (open_tm_rec k' u t1) T; lc gives lc_tm_at K k t1' and lc_ty_at K T; IH; lc_tm_tapp ✓.

`lc_close` (the substitution-preserving lc lemma):

```
Lemma has_type_close_lc : forall Δ Γ t T, has_type Δ Γ t T ->
  forall σ γ, (forall X, lc_ty_at 0 (σ X)) -> (forall x, locally_closed_tm (γ x)) ->

Induction on H:
- T_Var: close σ γ (tm_fvar x) = tm_subst_env γ (tm_ty_subst_env σ (tm_fvar x)) = tm_subst_env γ (tm_fvar x) = γ x. Simp. Apply Hγ.
- T_Abs: t = tm_abs T1 t2. close = tm_abs (ty_subst_env σ T1) (close σ γ t2). Need lc_tm_at 0 0 => lc_ty_at 0 (ty_subst_env σ T1) and lc_tm_at 0 1 (close σ γ t2).

  * lc_tm 0 1: pick x := fresh (L ++ fv_tm t2). Hmm, the IH for the premise is: forall x, ~In x L -> forall σ γ, ... -> locally_closed_tm (close σ γ (open_tm t2 (tm_fvar x))). We instantiate x fresh.
    Choose γ' := fun y => if Nat.eqb y x then tm_fvar x else γ y. Then:
    - γ' lc: for y=x, tm_fvar x lc; else γ y lc ✓.

  Pick X := fresh (L ++ fv_tm_ty t1). IH at X: forall σ γ, lc σ -> lc γ -> locally_closed_tm (close σ γ (open_tm_ty t1 (fvar-ty X))).

- T_Choice: IH both, lc_tm_choice ✓.

Note: `locally_closed_tm` = lc_tm_at 0 0, and pattern matching on it requires unfolding; using `simpl` or `unfold locally_closed_tm` etc.

Also, we need the lemma `fv_tm_ty_subst_env : fv_tm (tm_ty_subst_env σ t) = fv_tm t` and `fv_ty_ty_subst_env : fv_ty (ty_subst_env σ T) = fv_ty T`? For our agreement lemma for tm_ty_subst_env, we need: if σ1 and σ2 agree on fv_tm_ty t, then tm_ty_subst_env σ1 t = tm_ty_subst_env σ2 t. For the type-substitution agreement: `tm_ty_subst_env_agree : (forall X, In X (fv_tm_ty t) -> σ1 X = σ2 X) -> tm_ty_subst_env σ1 t = tm_ty_subst_env σ2 t`, proven together with `ty_subst_env_agree`. Under FVar X: need σ1 X = σ2 X from In X [X] ✓. ✓.

  Actually let's carefully write:
  close σi γi' (open_tm t2 (fvar x))

### The multi-step value lemma and V-relatedness

Let's think about the T_App proof in detail.

IH1 : C (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) δ ρ q1 q2 where q_i := close σi γi t1.
IH2 : C T1 δ ρ r1 r2 where r_i := close σi γi t2.
Unfold C: q1 -->* v1, q2 -->* v2, V (T1→T2) δ ρ v1 v2; r1 -->* w1, r2 -->* w2, V T1 δ ρ w1 w2.
V arrow: value v1, value v2, v1 = tm_abs Ta b1, v2 = tm_abs Tb b2, and forall a1 a2, V T1 δ ρ a1 a2 -> ∃ u1 u2, tm_app v1 a1 -->* u1 ∧ tm_app v2 a2 -->* u2 ∧ V T2 δ ρ u1 u2.
Apply with a1 := w1, a2 := w2: get u1,u2, `tm_app v1 w1 -->* u1`, `tm_app v2 w2 -->* u2`, V T2 δ ρ u1 u2.
Goal: C T2 δ ρ (tm_app q1 r1) (tm_app q2 r2) = ∃ u1' u2', tm_app q1 r1 -->* u1' ∧ ... ∧ V T2 ...
Chain: tm_app q1 r1 -->* tm_app v1 r1 [multi_app_l q1-->*v1 with lc r1] -->* tm_app v1 w1 [multi_app_r value v1 r1-->*w1] -->* u1 [given].
So u1 is the witness. Similarly u2. Need lc r1: from has_type_close_lc on the subderivation of t2 with σ1,γ1. ✓ (we have the subderivation has_type Δ Γ t2 T1).
Wait, but the term q1 = close σ1 γ1 t1 with t1 : T1→T2, and r1 = close σ1 γ1 t2. The lc lemma gives lc of close σ1 γ1 t2. ✓.

Also, `value v1` comes from V arrow. `multi_app_r` needs value v1 ✓.

- T_Abs proof detail: the goal is C (T1→T2) δ ρ A1 A2 where Ai = close σi γi (tm_abs T1 t2) = tm_abs (ty_subst_env σi T1) (close σi γi t2) (by simpl). We take v1=A1, v2=A2, refl. V arrow needs:
  * value Ai: from has_type_close_lc applied to the whole T_Abs derivation? The whole term is `has_type Δ Γ (tm_abs T1 t2) (T1→T2)`; close σi γi (tm_abs T1 t2) is lc ✓. So value (tm_abs ...) via v_abs with the lc. But V arrow expects `value v1` where v1 = Ai; we can constructor v_abs. ✓.
  * shape: exists ... Ai = tm_abs ...; Ai is definitionally tm_abs (ty_subst_env σi T1) (close ...). Provide witnesses.
  * forall a1 a2 V T1 ...: as detailed. Need value a1/value a2 via V_value (with wf δ ρ). Then ST_AppAbs with lc Ai and value a1. ✓.
    Chain: `tm_app Ai ai --> open_tm (close σi γi t2) ai` (ST_AppAbs; note Ai's annotation is ty_subst_env σi T1 and body close σi γi t2, so open_tm gives open_tm (close σi γi t2) ai ✓).
    IH at fresh x gives C T2 δ ρ (open_tm (close σ1 γ1 t2) a1) (open_tm (close σ2 γ2 t2) a2), which unfolds to ∃u1 u2, open...-->*u1 ... V T2 u1 u2. So the V arrow's existential holds with these. ✓.
  * Need the IH's environment condition: discussed.
  * The IH's σ/γ lc hypotheses.
  * Note the IH is applied with the *same* σi, γi' etc. The IH's conclusion after commutation must match exactly the terms in the goal. We proved the commutation gives `open_tm (close σi γi t2) ai`. ✓.

- T_TApp proof detail:
  IH1 : C (Ty_All T0) δ ρ q1 q2, qi = close σi γi t1.
  Unfold: q1 -->* v1, q2 -->* v2, V (Ty_All T0) δ ρ v1 v2.
  V_All gives value v1, v2, s1,s2 with v1=tm_tabs s1, v2=tm_tabs s2, and forall U1 U2 lc R value-rel, ∃w1 w2, open_tm_ty s1 U1 -->* w1 ∧ open_tm_ty s2 U2 -->* w2 ∧ V T0 (extend_b R δ) ρ w1 w2.

  Then V (open_ty T0 U0) δ ρ w1 w2 follows from V_open_ty_rec with U := U0, k := 0: V (open_ty_rec 0 U0 T0) δ ρ ↔ V T0 (fun i a b => if i =? 0 then V U0 δ ρ a b else δ i a b) ρ = V T0 (extend_b R δ) ρ. ✓.
  Reductions: `tm_tapp q1 U1' -->* tm_tapp v1 U1'` [multi_tapp with lc U1'] `--> tm_tapp (tm_tabs s1) U1'` — hmm, multi_tapp ends at `tm_tapp v1 U1'`; then the single step ST_TAppTabs: `tm_tapp (tm_tabs s1) U1' --> open_tm_ty s1 U1'`, using lc (tm_tabs s1) and lc U1'. lc (tm_tabs s1): value v1 and v1 = tm_tabs s1 → value (tm_tabs s1) → lc. ✓. Then `-->* w1`. ✓. So the chain gives the needed reduction. Similarly for the right.
  Thus C holds with witnesses w1,w2.
  Need lc U1': wf_ty Δ U0 -> lc_ty_at 0 U0; ty_subst_env_lc with σi lc. ✓.
  Need lc (tm_tabs s1): from value v1 = tm_tabs s1; inversion of value gives lc. We can prove value → locally_closed_tm and rewrite. ✓.

- T_Choice: goal C T δ ρ (tm_choice q1 q2) (tm_choice q1' q2') where the IH1 gives C T δ ρ q1 q1' and IH2 gives C T δ ρ q2 q2'. Since choice steps to either branch, we can just use the first branch's relation! `tm_choice q1 q2 --> q1` via ST_ChoiceLeft (needs lc q1 and lc q2), then q1 -->* v1 etc. So C T with witnesses from IH1. Need lc q1, q2: from has_type_close_lc on the subderivations t1 and t2 (the choice's premises). ✓. Actually, we only need lc q1 and q2 for the ST_ChoiceLeft premise (both). ✓.
  Hmm wait: the goal's left term is close σ1 γ1 (tm_choice t1 t2) = tm_choice (close σ1 γ1 t1) (close σ1 γ1 t2) and the right is tm_choice (close σ2 γ2 t1) (close σ2 γ2 t2). Use ST_ChoiceLeft on the left: tm_choice q1 q2 --> q1; then q1 -->* v1 and q1' -->* v2' (from IH1's C: C T δ ρ q1 q1' gives q1 -->* v1, q1' -->* v2, V T ...). Chain the left: tm_choice q1 q2 --> q1 -->* v1. The right: tm_choice q1' q2' --> q1' -->* v2. So C T δ ρ (tm_choice ...) (tm_choice ...) with witnesses v1,v2 and V from IH1. ✓. lc q1, q2 needed for the step; we get from has_type_close_lc on the premises. ✓.

- T_Var: ✓.
- T_TApp: ✓.
- T_Abs: ✓.
- T_TAbs: ✓.

Great. Now, an important check: the fundamental theorem's statement quantifies σ1,σ2 and requires `∀X, lc_ty_at 0 (σi X)`. But in the T_TAbs case, σi' = upd σi X Ui, where Ui comes from V_All's quantifier with lc_ty_at 0 Ui. So σi' lc at every atom ✓. In the T_TApp case, σ unchanged. In the T_Abs case, σ unchanged. ✓.

Now, the induction on has_type in Coq: the `T_Abs` constructor has a premise `forall x, ~In x L -> has_type ...`; the IH will be a forall over x with the freshness condition and then the σ,γ... arguments. We'll need to instantiate. Similarly T_TAbs.

Let me now think about how to structure the Coq proof to be manageable. This is a large proof. I'll write it incrementally and compile often.

First, let me set up the file with the given definitions plus my additions, then compile the syntactic lemmas. Let me start coding.

Actually, before coding, let me reconsider the V definition's arrow case: I require `value v1 /\ value v2 /\ (exists T1' b1, v1 = tm_abs T1' b1) /\ ...`. In the V_All's body relation at type arrow(bvar0)(bvar0), w1,w2 must be tm_abs values. ✓.

One concern: In the final theorem, we need `V (Ty_Arrow (Ty_BVar 0) (Ty_BVar 0)) (extend_b R zero) ρ w1 w2` and then apply the forall with a1=a2=v. The V arrow's condition is `forall a1 a2, V (Ty_BVar 0) ... a1 a2 -> ...`. V (Ty_BVar 0) (extend_b R δ) ρ a1 a2 = extend_b R δ 0 a1 a2 = R a1 a2. ✓ (definitional).

Now, let me also double check the `V_open_ty_rec` use in T_TApp: U0 is the source type argument, which must satisfy lc_ty_at 0 U0. We get wf_ty Δ U0 → lc_ty_at 0 U0. ✓.

But wait: the fundamental theorem's goal type in T_TApp is `open_ty T0 U0`; the semantics V is applied to this type. `open_ty T0 U0 = open_ty_rec 0 U0 T0`. ✓.

Another check: In the fundamental theorem's conclusion, the type index T can be a type with free fvars (from Δ). The V semantics interprets them via ρ. The σ substitutions produce terms but not types. The relation between the semantics of T and the actual substitutions σ is not enforced, which is fine because the term relation is untyped and the typing derivation ensures lc. ```coq
(* ====== Auxiliary definitions ====== *)

Definition rel := tm -> tm -> Prop.
Definition bvar_env := nat -> rel.
Definition fvar_env := atom -> rel.
Definition tm_env := atom -> tm.
Definition ty_env := atom -> ty.

Definition extend_b (R : rel) (delta : bvar_env) : bvar_env :=
  fun i => match i with 0 => R | S j => delta j end.

Definition upd_f (rho : fvar_env) (X : atom) (R : rel) : fvar_env :=
  fun Y => if Nat.eqb Y X then R else rho Y.

Definition upd_ty (sigma : ty_env) (X : atom) (U : ty) : ty_env :=
  fun Y => if Nat.eqb Y X then U else sigma Y.

Definition upd_tm (gamma : tm_env) (x : atom) (u : tm) : tm_env :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb y x then u else gamma y.

Fixpoint ty_subst_env (sigma : ty_env) (T : ty) : ty := ...
Fixpoint tm_ty_subst_env ...
Fixpoint tm_subst_env ...

Definition close (sigma : ty_env) (gamma : tm_env) (t : tm) : tm :=
  tm_subst_env gamma (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t).
```

Wait: `upd_f` returns a rel; notation issues: `fun Y => if ... then R else rho Y` — R is a rel (a function tm -> tm -> Prop), and `rho Y` likewise, so the if returns a function; OK, but Coq's `if` on functions works (it's just a match). Actually `Nat.eqb Y X` returns bool, so `if ... then R else rho Y` is fine (both rel).

Similarly `upd_ty`.

`extend_b`: `match i with 0 => R | S j => delta j end` returns rel.

Now `V`:

```coq
Fixpoint V (T : ty) (delta : bvar_env) (rho : fvar_env) (v1 v2 : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_BVar i => delta i v1 v2
  | Ty_FVar X => rho X v1 v2
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      value v1 /\ value v2 /\
      (exists T1' b1, v1 = tm_abs T1' b1) /\
      (exists T2' b2, v2 = tm_abs T2' b2) /\
      (forall a1 a2, V T1 delta rho a1 a2 ->
         exists w1 w2,
           tm_app v1 a1 -->* w1 /\
           tm_app v2 a2 -->* w2 /\
           V T2 delta rho w1 w2)
  | Ty_All T' =>
      value v1 /\ value v2 /\
      (exists s1 s2,
         v1 = tm_tabs s1 /\ v2 = tm_tabs s2 /\
         forall U1 U2,
           lc_ty_at 0 U1 -> lc_ty_at 0 U2 ->
           forall R, (forall x1 x2, R x1 x2 -> value x1 /\ value x2) ->
             exists w1 w2,
               open_tm_ty s1 U1 -->* w1 /\
               open_tm_ty s2 U2 -->* w2 /\
               V T' (extend_b R delta) rho w1 w2)
  end.
```

Hmm, for the All case I could simplify by not requiring `value v1` separately but the lc... Let's keep as is; but note the `exists s1 s2, v1 = tm_tabs s1 /\ ...` and `value v1`: from value v1 and v1=tm_tabs s1 we can get lc.

  exists v1 v2, t1 -->* v1 /\ t2 -->* v2 /\ V T delta rho v1 v2.

`V_delta_iff`:

Lemma V_delta_iff : forall T delta1 delta2 rho v1 v2,
  (forall i x1 x2, delta1 i x1 x2 <-> delta2 i x1 x2) ->
  V T delta1 rho v1 v2 <-> V T delta2 rho v1 v2.

  induction T; simpl; intros; try apply H; try tauto;
  ...
```
Hmm, the arrow case has multiple components; `tauto` might handle it if the IH reproduces the same structure? The goal is `(A /\ B /\ C /\ D /\ E) ↔ (A /\ B /\ C /\ D /\ E')` where E and E' differ by the relation. `tauto` cannot use the induction hypothesis as a lemma unless we assert it. We'll do:
```
  - (* Arrow *) 
    assert (H1 := IHT1 delta1 delta2 rho); assert (H2 := IHT2 ...).
```
Actually the IH after `induction T` is `forall delta1 delta2 rho v1 v2, ... -> V T1 ... ↔ ...` for each subterm T1. So we can specialize.

Better to write the proof with explicit destruct/split. Let me think about a generic tactic:
```
Lemma V_delta_iff : forall T, forall delta1 delta2 rho v1 v2,
  (forall i x1 x2, delta1 i x1 x2 <-> delta2 i x1 x2) ->
  V T delta1 rho v1 v2 <-> V T delta2 rho v1 v2.
Proof.
  induction T as [i|X|T1 IH1 T2 IH2|T' IH]; intros delta1 delta2 rho v1 v2 Hagree; simpl.
  - apply Hagree.
  - reflexivity.
  - split; intros (Hv1 & Hv2 & Hsh1 & Hsh2 & Hforall);
      repeat split; auto;
      intros a1 a2 Ha;
      [ specialize (Hforall a1 a2 (proj1 (IH1 _ _ _ _ _ Hagree) Ha)) | ... ];
      destruct Hforall as (w1 & w2 & Hr1 & Hr2 & HV);
      exists w1, w2; repeat split; auto;
      apply (proj1 (IH2 ...)) in HV; auto.
```
Hmm, the direction handling gets verbose. `repeat split; auto` may work for conjunctions but the forall needs both directions. Let me write:
```
  - (* arrow *)
    assert (E1 : forall a1 a2, V T1 delta1 rho a1 a2 <-> V T1 delta2 rho a1 a2).
    { intros; apply IH1; auto. }
    assert (E2 : forall w1 w2, V T2 delta1 rho w1 w2 <-> V T2 delta2 rho w1 w2).
    { intros; apply IH2; auto. }
    split; intros (Hv1 & Hv2 & Hsh1 & Hsh2 & Hf); repeat split; auto;
      intros a1 a2 Ha; specialize (Hf a1 a2); 
      [ apply E1 in Ha | apply E1 in Ha ] ...
```
Wait, IH1's statement after induction: `IH1 : forall delta1 delta2 rho v1 v2, (forall i x1 x2, ...) -> V T1 delta1 rho v1 v2 <-> V T1 delta2 rho v1 v2`. So `IH1 delta1 delta2 rho a1 a2 Hagree`. ✓.

Let me write a helper:
```
    assert (E1 : forall a1 a2, V T1 delta1 rho a1 a2 <-> V T1 delta2 rho a1 a2)
      by (intros; apply IH1; auto).
```
Then:
```
    split.
    + intros (Hv1 & Hv2 & Hsh1 & Hsh2 & Hf). repeat split; auto.
      intros a1 a2 Ha. apply E1 in Ha. destruct (Hf a1 a2 Ha) as (w1 & w2 & Hr1 & Hr2 & HV).
      exists w1, w2. repeat split; auto. apply E2; auto.
    + intros (Hv1 & Hv2 & Hsh1 & Hsh2 & Hf). repeat split; auto.
      intros a1 a2 Ha. apply E1 in Ha. destruct (Hf a1 a2 Ha) as (w1 & w2 & Hr1 & Hr2 & HV).
      exists w1, w2. repeat split; auto. apply E2; auto.
```
Wait, in the first direction, `apply E1 in Ha` transforms Ha : V T1 delta1 into V T1 delta2 (since E1 is an iff); Hf expects V T1 delta1. Oops, direction: Hf : forall a1 a2, V T1 delta1 ... -> ... . Given Ha : V T1 delta1, we need it as is; for the result HV : V T2 delta1, we need E2 to convert to delta2. So no `apply E1 in Ha`. In the second direction, Hf expects delta2 and the result needs conversion from delta2 to delta1. So:
```
    + ... destruct (Hf a1 a2 Ha) ... HV; ... apply E2; auto.
    + ... destruct (Hf a1 a2 Ha) ... HV; ... apply E2; auto.  (* E2 : V T2 delta1 <-> V T2 delta2 *)
```
Hmm, in the second direction, Ha : V T1 delta2, Hf expects V T1 delta2 ✓; HV : V T2 delta2; goal V T2 delta1 → apply E2; auto uses the iff: goal is V T2 delta1, `apply E2` changes goal to V T2 delta2 (since E2's forward direction is delta1→delta2; applying an iff as a lemma: `apply E2` with goal V T2 delta1 will use the backward direction ✓, producing V T2 delta2). In the first direction, HV : V T2 delta1, goal V T2 delta2: `apply E2; auto` uses forward direction ✓.

OK, manageable.

    [ specialize (Hf U1 U2 HCl1 HCl2 R HR) | ... ];

`V_value`:
```
Lemma V_value : forall T delta rho v1 v2,
  (forall i x1 x2, delta i x1 x2 -> value x1 /\ value x2) ->
  (forall X x1 x2, rho X x1 x2 -> value x1 /\ value x2) ->
  V T delta rho v1 v2 -> value v1 /\ value v2.

`V_open_ty_rec`:
```
Lemma V_open_ty_rec : forall T k U delta rho v1 v2,
  lc_ty_at 0 U ->
  (V (open_ty_rec k U T) delta rho v1 v2 <->
   V T (fun i a b => if Nat.eqb i k then V U delta rho a b else delta i a b) rho v1 v2).
```
Proof induction on T, K generalized? The lemma's statement has k arbitrary and T; the All case recurses on T' with k+1, so the induction is on T with k as a parameter (generalized in the proof). In Coq: `induction T as [i|X|T1 IH1 T2 IH2|T' IH]; intros k U delta rho v1 v2 Hlc; simpl.`

- BVar i: `simpl`. The goal: `V (if i =? k then U else Ty_BVar i) delta rho v1 v2 ↔ V (Ty_BVar i) (fun i a b => ...) rho v1 v2`. Note V (Ty_BVar i) ... = env' i v1 v2. Simplify: `destruct (Nat.eqb i k) eqn:E; simpl; reflexivity.` Let's see: LHS when true: V U delta rho v1 v2; RHS: (fun i a b => if i=?k then V U delta rho a b else delta i a b) i v1 v2 = if i=?k then V U ... else delta i ... = V U ... (using E). With `simpl` and rewriting E, should be reflexivity. When false: LHS = delta i v1 v2; RHS = delta i v1 v2 ✓. Use `destruct (Nat.eqb i k) eqn:E; simpl; reflexivity.` But careful: after destruct, the RHS env's `Nat.eqb i k` occurrence should compute using the equation. If we `destruct (Nat.eqb i k)` before `simpl`, both occurrences are replaced. The LHS's `if` is inside open_ty_rec which got simplified in the goal via `simpl`? We need to control unfolding. Let's write the BVar case explicitly:
  ```
  - cbn [open_ty_rec V]. destruct (Nat.eqb i k); cbn; reflexivity.
  ```
  Hmm, `open_ty_rec` is a Fixpoint on T; for T = Ty_BVar i, `cbn` should reduce `open_ty_rec k U (Ty_BVar i)` to `if Nat.eqb k i then U else Ty_BVar i`. Note the order of arguments: `open_ty_rec (k : nat) (U T : ty)`: `if Nat.eqb k i`. And our env is `if Nat.eqb i k`. `Nat.eqb` is symmetric logically but not definitionally; `Nat.eqb k i` vs `Nat.eqb i k` — for nats, eqb is symmetric propositionally, but to `destruct` both we need them to be the same term. We can use `Nat.eqb_neq`/`Nat.eqb_eq` or rewrite `Nat.eqb_sym`? Is there a lemma `Nat.eqb_sym : forall a b, (a =? b) = (b =? a)`? There's `Nat.eqb_sym` in PeanoNat? Let me check: Coq's PeanoNat has `Nat.eqb_sym : forall a b, (a =? b) = (b =? a)`. I believe there is `Nat.eqb_sym`. We can use `rewrite (Nat.eqb_sym k i)` or just destruct on `Nat.eqb k i` and separately on `Nat.eqb i k` with case analysis; simpler: define our env with `Nat.eqb k i` too? In the lemma statement, we can write the env as `fun i a b => if Nat.eqb k i then V U delta rho a b else delta i a b`, matching open_ty_rec's order. And in the All case arithmetic, handle `Nat.eqb (S k) (S j)` vs `Nat.eqb k j` which is definitionally true (`Nat.eqb (S a) (S b)` reduces to `Nat.eqb a b`? Let's check the definition of Nat.eqb: `Fixpoint eqb n m := match n, m with 0,0 => true | S n, S m => eqb n m | _, _ => false end.` So `Nat.eqb (S k) (S j)` reduces to `Nat.eqb k j` by computation (cbn). ✓. And `Nat.eqb 0 (S j) = false`, `Nat.eqb (S j) 0 = false` by computation. So using `Nat.eqb k i` (constant first) helps in the All case: the env index i ranges; at i=0: `Nat.eqb (k+1) 0` = false; at i=S j: `Nat.eqb (k+1) (S j)` = `Nat.eqb k j`. ✓.

  But in the `replace_at`-style env, we also need to consider the base case where open_ty_rec checks `Nat.eqb k i` and the env checks `Nat.eqb k i` (same order) ✓.

Let me draft the file content additions:

```coq
(* ============ Auxiliary definitions ============ *)

Definition rel : Type := tm -> tm -> Prop.
Definition bvar_env : Type := nat -> rel.
Definition fvar_env : Type := atom -> rel.
Definition tm_env : Type := atom -> tm.
Definition ty_env : Type := atom -> ty.

Definition extend_b (R : rel) (delta : bvar_env) : bvar_env :=
  fun i => match i with 0 => R | S j => delta j end.

Definition upd_b (k : nat) (R : rel) (delta : bvar_env) : bvar_env :=
  fun i => if Nat.eqb k i then R else delta i.

Definition upd_f (rho : fvar_env) (X : atom) (R : rel) : fvar_env :=
  fun Y => if Nat.eqb Y X then R else rho Y.

Definition upd_ty (sigma : ty_env) (X : atom) (U : ty) : ty_env :=
  fun Y => if Nat.eqb Y X then U else sigma Y.

Definition upd_tm (gamma : tm_env) (x : atom) (u : tm) : tm_env :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb y x then u else gamma y.

Fixpoint ty_subst_env (sigma : ty_env) (T : ty) : ty :=
  match T with
  | Ty_BVar i => Ty_BVar i
  | Ty_FVar X => sigma X
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => Ty_Arrow (ty_subst_env sigma T1) (ty_subst_env sigma T2)
  | Ty_All T1 => Ty_All (ty_subst_env sigma T1)
  end.

Fixpoint tm_ty_subst_env (sigma : ty_env) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => tm_fvar x
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs (ty_subst_env sigma T) (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t1)
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t1) (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t2)
  | tm_tabs t1 => tm_tabs (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t1)
  | tm_tapp t1 T => tm_tapp (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t1) (ty_subst_env sigma T)
  | tm_choice t1 t2 => tm_choice (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t1) (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t2)
  end.

Fixpoint tm_subst_env (gamma : tm_env) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => gamma x
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (tm_subst_env gamma t1)
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (tm_subst_env gamma t1) (tm_subst_env gamma t2)
  | tm_tabs t1 => tm_tabs (tm_subst_env gamma t1)
  | tm_tapp t1 T => tm_tapp (tm_subst_env gamma t1) T
  | tm_choice t1 t2 => tm_choice (tm_subst_env gamma t1) (tm_subst_env gamma t2)
  end.

Definition close (sigma : ty_env) (gamma : tm_env) (t : tm) : tm :=
  tm_subst_env gamma (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t).
```

Free variable functions:
```coq
Fixpoint fv_ty (T : ty) : list atom :=
  match T with
  | Ty_BVar _ => []
  | Ty_FVar X => [X]
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => fv_ty T1 ++ fv_ty T2
  | Ty_All T1 => fv_ty T1
  end.

Fixpoint fv_tm_ty (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => []
  | tm_fvar _ => []
  | tm_abs T t1 => fv_ty T ++ fv_tm_ty t1
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv_tm_ty t1 ++ fv_tm_ty t2
  | tm_tabs t1 => fv_tm_ty t1
  | tm_tapp t1 T => fv_tm_ty t1 ++ fv_ty T
  | tm_choice t1 t2 => fv_tm_ty t1 ++ fv_tm_ty t2
  end.

Fixpoint fv_tm (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => []
  | tm_fvar x => [x]
  | tm_abs T t1 => fv_tm t1
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv_tm t1 ++ fv_tm t2
  | tm_tabs t1 => fv_tm t1
  | tm_tapp t1 T => fv_tm t1
  | tm_choice t1 t2 => fv_tm t1 ++ fv_tm t2
  end.
```

Freshness:
```coq
Fixpoint max_list (l : list nat) : nat :=
  match l with
  | [] => 0
  | x :: l' => Nat.max x (max_list l')
  end.

Lemma max_list_ge : forall l x, In x l -> x <= max_list l.
Proof.
  induction l as [|y l IH]; intros x Hin; simpl in *.
  - contradiction.
  - destruct Hin as [-> | Hin].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + eapply Nat.le_trans; [apply IH; eauto | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Definition fresh (l : list atom) : atom := S (max_list l).

Lemma fresh_not_in : forall l, ~ In (fresh l) l.
Proof.
  intros l Hin. unfold fresh in Hin. apply max_list_ge in Hin. lia.
Qed.
```

Lemma eqb_false : forall a b, a <> b -> Nat.eqb a b = false.
Proof. intros; apply Nat.eqb_neq; auto. Qed.

Lemma eqb_true : forall a b, a = b -> Nat.eqb a b = true.
Proof. intros; subst; apply Nat.eqb_refl. Qed.
```
Actually `Nat.eqb_eq : forall a b, (a =? b) = true <-> a = b` and `Nat.eqb_neq : forall a b, (a =? b) = false <-> a <> b`. Good.

```coq
Lemma lc_ty_weaken : forall K K' T, K <= K' -> lc_ty_at K T -> lc_ty_at K' T.
Proof.
  intros K K' T Hle H. induction H; auto using lc_ty_at.
  - apply lc_ty_bvar; lia.
  - apply lc_ty_arrow; auto.
  - apply lc_ty_all; auto.
Qed.
```
Hmm, `induction H` on lc_ty_at K T: cases:
- lc_ty_bvar i Hi: goal lc_ty_at K' (Ty_BVar i); apply lc_ty_bvar; lia.
- lc_ty_fvar: apply lc_ty_fvar.
- lc_ty_arrow: IH1, IH2; lc_ty_arrow.
- lc_ty_all: IH; lc_ty_all. Note the IH is for lc_ty_at (S K) T1 and the goal lc_ty_at K' (Ty_All T1) needs lc_ty_at (S K') T1; the IH has statement for the same K,K'? The induction hypothesis after `induction H` where H : lc_ty_at K T: for the All case, IH : forall K K', K<=K' -> lc_ty_at K T1 -> lc_ty_at K' T1? Hmm, `induction H` with K,K',Hle in context: Coq generalizes? `induction H` will give IHs for the premises, but K,K' are fixed; for the All case, the premise is lc_ty_at (S K) T1, and the IH would be `K <= K' -> lc_ty_at (S K) T1 -> lc_ty_at (S K') T1`? I'm not sure the induction principle will have the right K'. Better to prove by induction on T instead:
```coq
Lemma lc_ty_weaken : forall K K' T, K <= K' -> lc_ty_at K T -> lc_ty_at K' T.
Proof.
  intros K K' T Hle H. revert K' Hle. induction H; intros K' Hle; auto using lc_ty_at.
  - apply lc_ty_bvar; lia.
Qed.
```
After `induction H`, the All case IH: `forall K', S K <= K' -> lc_ty_at K' T1`? Hmm, the All constructor premise is lc_ty_at (S K) T1, and the induction principle of lc_ty_at gives IH as a function of the premise. Let me just write it explicitly with `induction H`; test in Coq.

Similarly:
```coq
Lemma lc_tm_weaken_K : forall K K' k t, K <= K' -> lc_tm_at K k t -> lc_tm_at K' k t.
Lemma lc_tm_weaken_k : forall K k k' t, k <= k' -> lc_tm_at K k t -> lc_tm_at K' k t.
```
Careful with statement: `lc_tm_weaken_k : forall K k k' t, k <= k' -> lc_tm_at K k t -> lc_tm_at K k' t`.

And a combined:
`lc_tm_weaken : forall K K' k k' t, K <= K' -> k <= k' -> lc_tm_at K k t -> lc_tm_at K' k' t`.

Since value's constructors both have the lc premise. `destruct H` gives `locally_closed_tm (tm_abs T t)` and `locally_closed_tm (tm_tabs t)`, which are the goals. ✓.

Now, the `tm_ty_subst_env_agree` lemma:
```coq
Lemma ty_subst_env_agree : forall sigma1 sigma2 T,
  (forall X, In X (fv_ty T) -> sigma1 X = sigma2 X) ->
  ty_subst_env sigma1 T = ty_subst_env sigma2 T.
Lemma tm_ty_subst_env_agree : forall sigma1 sigma2 t,
  (forall X, In X (fv_tm_ty t) -> sigma1 X = sigma2 X) ->
  tm_ty_subst_env sigma1 t = tm_ty_subst_env sigma2 t.
Lemma tm_subst_env_agree : forall gamma1 gamma2 t,
  (forall x, In x (fv_tm t) -> gamma1 x = gamma2 x) ->
  tm_subst_env gamma1 t = tm_subst_env gamma2 t.
```
Proof by induction. For tm_ty_subst_env, the abs case needs ty_subst_env_agree and IH; the In conditions: fv_tm_ty (tm_abs T t1) = fv_ty T ++ fv_tm_ty t1; use In_app. Similarly tapp. For tm_subst_env, fvar case uses H with In x [x]. ✓.

And `fv_tm_ty_subst_env : fv_tm (tm_ty_subst_env σ t) = fv_tm t`. Actually we need fv_tm of the substituted term to compare γ' and γ. Let's prove `fv_tm_ty_subst_env : forall σ t, fv_tm (tm_ty_subst_env σ t) = fv_tm t`. Induction t; tm_abs: fv_tm (tm_abs (subst T) (subst t1)) = fv_tm (subst t1) = fv_tm t1 (IH) = fv_tm (tm_abs T t1) ✓. tapp: fv_tm (subst t1) = fv_tm t1 ✓ (the type arg doesn't contribute term fvars). ✓.

Now the big part: the logical relation V, C, semantic lemmas, fundamental theorem.

Let me add the semantics and semantic lemmas first.

Definitions:

```coq
Fixpoint V (T : ty) (delta : bvar_env) (rho : fvar_env) (v1 v2 : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_BVar i => delta i v1 v2
  | Ty_FVar X => rho X v1 v2
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      value v1 /\ value v2 /\
      (exists T1' b1, v1 = tm_abs T1' b1) /\
      (exists T2' b2, v2 = tm_abs T2' b2) /\
      (forall a1 a2, V T1 delta rho a1 a2 ->
         exists w1 w2,
           tm_app v1 a1 -->* w1 /\
           tm_app v2 a2 -->* w2 /\
           V T2 delta rho w1 w2)
  | Ty_All T' =>
      value v1 /\ value v2 /\
      (exists s1 s2,
         v1 = tm_tabs s1 /\ v2 = tm_tabs s2 /\
         forall U1 U2,
           lc_ty_at 0 U1 -> lc_ty_at 0 U2 ->
           forall R, (forall x1 x2, R x1 x2 -> value x1 /\ value x2) ->
             exists w1 w2,
               open_tm_ty s1 U1 -->* w1 /\
               open_tm_ty s2 U2 -->* w2 /\
               V T' (extend_b R delta) rho w1 w2)
  end.
```

Concern: Coq may complain about the recursive calls because the All case's recursive call V T' is inside `exists s1 s2, ...` and under a `forall ... ->`; structurally it's a direct subterm, so fine.

Definition C:
```coq
Definition C (T : ty) (delta : bvar_env) (rho : fvar_env) (t1 t2 : tm) : Prop :=
  exists v1 v2, t1 -->* v1 /\ t2 -->* v2 /\ V T delta rho v1 v2.
```

Lemmas:

1. `V_delta_iff`.
2. `V_rho_iff`.
3. `V_delta_indep`.
4. `V_rho_indep`.
5. `V_value`.
6. `V_open_ty_rec`.
7. `V_delta_upd_fresh`? maybe not needed if V_rho_indep used.
8. `C_rho_indep`, `C_delta_iff` as needed.
9. `C_value_refl`.

Let me think about what precisely the fundamental theorem needs:
- V_rho_indep to transfer the environment condition C from ρ to ρ' (with fv_ty Tx).
- V_delta_indep in T_TAbs to convert upd_b 0 R δ to extend_b R δ.
- V_open_ty_rec in T_TAbs (U=FVar X) and T_TApp (U=U0).
- V_value for the arguments and R value-relatedness.

Let me write V_delta_iff and V_rho_iff first.

For V_delta_iff:
```coq
Lemma V_delta_iff : forall T (delta1 delta2 : bvar_env) (rho : fvar_env) v1 v2,
  (forall i x1 x2, delta1 i x1 x2 <-> delta2 i x1 x2) ->
  V T delta1 rho v1 v2 <-> V T delta2 rho v1 v2.
Proof.
  induction T as [i|X|T1 IH1 T2 IH2|T' IH];
    intros delta1 delta2 rho v1 v2 Hagree; simpl.
  - apply Hagree.
  - reflexivity.
  - assert (E1 : forall a1 a2, V T1 delta1 rho a1 a2 <-> V T1 delta2 rho a1 a2)
      by (intros; apply IH1; auto).
    assert (E2 : forall w1 w2, V T2 delta1 rho w1 w2 <-> V T2 delta2 rho w1 w2)
      by (intros; apply IH2; auto).
    split; intros (Hv1 & Hv2 & Hsh1 & Hsh2 & Hf);
      repeat split; auto;
      intros a1 a2 Ha;
      destruct (Hf a1 a2 Ha) as (w1 & w2 & Hr1 & Hr2 & HB);
      exists w1, w2; repeat split; auto;
      [ apply (proj2 (E2 w1 w2)) in HB | apply (proj1 (E2 w1 w2)) in HB ]; auto.
```
Hmm, direction: in the first split direction, delta1→delta2; Hf expects delta1 args, HB : V T2 delta1; goal V T2 delta2 → E2 forward: proj1 E2 w1 w2 : V T2 delta1 → V T2 delta2. So `apply (proj1 (E2 w1 w2)); auto`. In the second direction (delta2→delta1), Hf expects delta2 args (Ha : V T1 delta2), HB : V T2 delta2, goal V T2 delta1 → proj2. But the tactic script above applies to both directions; I need to distinguish. Coq's bullets: after `split; intros (...)`, there are two goals. The combined tactic `intros a1 a2 Ha; destruct ...` — for direction 1, `Hf a1 a2 Ha` needs Ha : V T1 delta1 ✓ (since delta1 is the LHS). For direction 2, Hf : forall a1 a2, V T1 delta2 ... -> ... and Ha : V T1 delta2 ✓. Good, no conversion needed for Ha. Then the final HV conversion differs by direction. So write two separate bullets.

Actually `split; intros H; ...` with H as a tuple: `intros (Hv1 & Hv2 & Hsh1 & Hsh2 & Hf)` works.

Let me write:
```
  - assert (E1 : ...) by ...
    assert (E2 : ...) by ...
    split.
    + intros (Hv1 & Hv2 & Hsh1 & Hsh2 & Hf). repeat split; auto.
      intros a1 a2 Ha. destruct (Hf a1 a2 Ha) as (w1 & w2 & Hr1 & Hr2 & HB).
      exists w1, w2. repeat split; auto. apply E2; auto.
    + intros (Hv1 & Hv2 & Hsh1 & Hsh2 & Hf). repeat split; auto.
      intros a1 a2 Ha. destruct (Hf a1 a2 Ha) as (w1 & w2 & Hr1 & Hr2 & HB).
      exists w1, w2. repeat split; auto. apply E2; auto.
```
Direction 1: goal after `apply E2; auto`? HB : V T2 delta1; goal V T2 delta2. `apply E2` with E2 : V T2 delta1 <-> V T2 delta2: applying an iff to goal V T2 delta2 uses the backward direction, giving V T2 delta1 — then auto uses HB. Hmm, we want forward. Actually the goal after `exists...; repeat split; auto` is `V T2 delta2 w1 w2` and HB : V T2 delta1 w1 w2. We want to use E2 forward: `exact (proj1 (E2 w1 w2) HB)`. Direction 2: goal V T2 delta1, HB : V T2 delta2: `exact (proj2 (E2 w1 w2) HB)`. So write them separately with exact.

For the All case:
```
  - assert (Hagree' : forall R i x1 x2, extend_b R delta1 i x1 x2 <-> extend_b R delta2 i x1 x2).
    { intros R [|j] x1 x2; simpl; auto. }
    assert (E : forall R w1 w2,
      V T' (extend_b R delta1) rho w1 w2 <-> V T' (extend_b R delta2) rho w1 w2)
      by (intros; apply IH; auto).
    split.
    + intros (Hv1 & Hv2 & s1 & s2 & Hs1 & Hs2 & Hf). repeat split; auto.
      exists s1, s2. repeat split; auto.
      intros U1 U2 Hl1 Hl2 R HR. destruct (Hf U1 U2 Hl1 Hl2 R HR) as (w1 & w2 & Hr1 & Hr2 & HB).
      exists w1, w2. repeat split; auto. exact (proj1 (E R w1 w2) HB).
    + ... exact (proj2 (E R w1 w2) HB).
```
Careful with the nesting of the exists and forall in the All case: V = value v1 /\ value v2 /\ exists s1 s2, v1 = tm_tabs s1 /\ v2 = tm_tabs s2 /\ forall U1 U2, lc -> lc -> forall R, VR -> exists w1 w2, ...
So destruct gives `(Hv1 & Hv2 & s1 & s2 & Hs1 & Hs2 & Hf)` — yes, since exists s1 s2 is nested inside a conjunction; the tuple pattern can peel existentials? The pattern `(Hv1 & Hv2 & s1 & s2 & Hs1 & Hs2 & Hf)` with `intros (...)` — Coq's destructing conjunction/existential patterns: `s1` in a tuple pattern is an existential witness, yes Coq supports intro patterns with existentials: `intros (a & b & c & ...)`. Here "&" is the destructing pattern separator for /\ and exists. I think it works.

Then the goal after split is the same shape; `repeat split; auto` will split the conjunctions, but the `exists s1 s2, ...` part: `repeat split` doesn't instantiate existentials automatically? Actually `split` on an existential goal does nothing (split applies to iff/and); use `exists s1, s2`. The `repeat split; auto` might leave the existential goal. Let me structure:
```
    + intros (Hv1 & Hv2 & s1 & s2 & Hs1 & Hs2 & Hf).
      split; [exact Hv1 | split; [exact Hv2 |]].
      exists s1, s2. split; [exact Hs1 | split; [exact Hs2 |]].
      intros U1 U2 Hl1 Hl2 R HR. ...
```
This is verbose but robust. Similarly for arrow:
```
    + intros (Hv1 & Hv2 & Hsh1 & Hsh2 & Hf).
      split; [exact Hv1 | split; [exact Hv2 | split; [exact Hsh1 | split; [exact Hsh2 |]]]].
      intros a1 a2 Ha. ...
```
Better to use `repeat split; auto` for the conjunction part but that fails on the final forall. Let me use a helper tactic: `destruct ... as (...)`; then `repeat split; auto` may leave the forall goal; then handle it. Actually `repeat split` splits the top-level conjunctions and also splits the *forall's conclusion*? No, split doesn't intro. After repeat split, remaining goals are the forall parts (the "and" of them). Then `auto` solves the simple ones and leaves the forall as goal; but if there are multiple goals, `auto` applies to all. It's fine; then the following intros applies to the remaining goal(s). Since there's exactly one remaining goal (the forall), we can do:
```
      repeat split; auto.
      intros a1 a2 Ha. ...
```
Hmm, but `repeat split; auto` — the `auto` part might fail? auto never fails. Then the `intros` applies to remaining goals; if only one, fine. If `repeat split` split the forall? It doesn't. OK.

Then V_delta_indep:
```coq
Lemma V_delta_indep : forall T K (delta1 delta2 : bvar_env) (rho : fvar_env) v1 v2,
  lc_ty_at K T ->
  (forall i, i < K -> forall x1 x2, delta1 i x1 x2 <-> delta2 i x1 x2) ->
  V T delta1 rho v1 v2 <-> V T delta2 rho v1 v2.
Proof.
  induction T as [i|X|T1 IH1 T2 IH2|T' IH];
    intros K delta1 delta2 rho v1 v2 Hlc Hagree; simpl.
  - inversion Hlc; subst. apply Hagree; auto.
  - reflexivity.
  - inversion Hlc; subst.
    assert (E1 : forall a1 a2, V T1 delta1 rho a1 a2 <-> V T1 delta2 rho a1 a2).
    { intros; apply IH1; auto. }
    assert (E2 : forall w1 w2, V T2 delta1 rho w1 w2 <-> V T2 delta2 rho w1 w2).
    { intros; apply IH2; auto. }
    split.
    + intros (Hv1 & Hv2 & Hsh1 & Hsh2 & Hf). repeat split; auto.
      intros a1 a2 Ha. destruct (Hf a1 a2 Ha) as (w1 & w2 & Hr1 & Hr2 & HB).
      exists w1, w2; repeat split; auto. exact (proj1 (E2 w1 w2) HB).
    + intros (...). ... exact (proj2 (E2 w1 w2) HB).
  - inversion Hlc; subst.
    assert (Hagree' : forall R i x1 x2, extend_b R delta1 i x1 x2 <-> extend_b R delta2 i x1 x2).
    { intros R [|j] x1 x2; simpl; auto. }
    ...
```
Hmm, for the All case, the IH is for T' with K := S K? The premise IH : forall K delta1 delta2 rho v1 v2, lc_ty_at K T' -> ... So apply IH with K := S K. The `auto` might find lc_ty_at (S K) T' and the agreement. Let me write `apply IH with (K := S K); auto.` — but the agreement for extend_b: `Hagree' R` provides for all i x1 x2; but the IH needs `forall i, i < S K -> ...`; auto with Hagree' should solve. Let me write:
```
    assert (E : forall R w1 w2, V T' (extend_b R delta1) rho w1 w2 <-> V T' (extend_b R delta2) rho w1 w2).
    { intros. apply IH with (K := S K); auto. }
```
The `auto` needs to prove `forall i, i < S K -> forall x1 x2, extend_b R delta1 i ... <-> ...`, using Hagree'. `auto` might not destruct i. Better:
```
    { intros R w1 w2. apply IH with (K := S K).
      - assumption.
      - intros i Hi x1 x2. destruct i as [|j]; simpl.
        + reflexivity.
        + apply Hagree. lia. }
```
Wait, Hargee in the All case's hypothes is `forall i, i < K -> ...`; for i = S j < S K we need j < K, lia ✓. And extend_b R delta1 (S j) = delta1 j ✓. For i=0 both R ✓.

Then the body of the All V uses E R w1 w2.

Note: after inversion Hlc; subst, the Hlc for the All case gives lc_ty_at (S K) T'. Is it exactly available? inversion of lc_ty_at K (Ty_All T') gives lc_ty_at (S K) T'. ✓.

V_rho_indep:
```coq
Lemma V_rho_indep : forall T (delta : bvar_env) (rho1 rho2 : fvar_env) v1 v2,
  (forall X, In X (fv_ty T) -> forall x1 x2, rho1 X x1 x2 <-> rho2 X x1 x2) ->
  V T delta rho1 v1 v2 <-> V T delta rho2 v1 v2.
```
Proof by induction T. Base FVar: apply Hagree with In [X]. BVar: reflexivity. Arrow: IH1/IH2; note IH1's hypothesis needs In X (fv_ty T1) → from Hagree and In_app. The IH is applied with the same delta and rho1/rho2: `apply IH1; intros X HX; apply Hagree; apply in_or_app; auto.` All: IH on T' with fv_ty T' = fv_ty (Ty_All T') (definitional); `apply IH; intros X HX; apply Hagree; simpl; exact HX`.

V_value:
```coq
Lemma V_value : forall T delta rho v1 v2,
  (forall i x1 x2, delta i x1 x2 -> value x1 /\ value x2) ->
  (forall X x1 x2, rho X x1 x2 -> value x1 /\ value x2) ->
  V T delta rho v1 v2 -> value v1 /\ value v2.
Proof.
  induction T as [i|X|T1 IH1 T2 IH2|T' IH]; intros delta rho v1 v2 Hd Hr HV; simpl in *.
  - apply Hd; auto.
  - apply Hr; auto.
  - destruct HV as (Hv1 & Hv2 & _). auto.
  - destruct HV as (Hv1 & Hv2 & _). auto.
Qed.
```
Note simpl in * may unfold V in HV; then HV has the conjunction; destruct works.

V_open_ty_rec as discussed. Let me write it carefully.

```coq
Lemma V_open_ty_rec : forall T k U (delta : bvar_env) (rho : fvar_env) v1 v2,
  lc_ty_at 0 U ->

   V T (upd_b k (V U delta rho) delta) rho v1 v2).

    intros k U delta rho v1 v2 Hlc; simpl.
  - (* BVar *)
    destruct (Nat.eqb k i) eqn:E; simpl.
    + reflexivity.
    + reflexivity.
```

- open_ty_rec k U (Ty_BVar i) = if Nat.eqb k i then U else Ty_BVar i.
- LHS V (...) delta rho v1 v2: if true → V U delta rho v1 v2; else delta i v1 v2.
- RHS V (Ty_BVar i) (upd_b k (V U delta rho) delta) rho v1 v2 = (upd_b k (V U delta rho) delta) i v1 v2 = if Nat.eqb k i then (V U delta rho) v1 v2 else delta i v1 v2.
After destruct E and simpl, both match. Does `simpl` unfold upd_b if given? `simpl` uses the definition of upd_b (a Definition with a fun body) — yes, simpl unfolds definitions only if they reduce (delta-reduction) when applied. `upd_b k X delta i` should reduce via beta/iota. Let me use `cbn` or `simpl`. I'll write `destruct (Nat.eqb k i) eqn:E; simpl; reflexivity.` and check.

Now, I also need a bridge lemma: `V_extend_upd0` for T_TAbs:
```coq
Lemma V_extend_upd0 : forall T delta rho R v1 v2,

  (V T (upd_b 0 R delta) rho v1 v2 <-> V T (extend_b R delta) rho v1 v2).

```

Actually the proof: `intros i Hi x1 x2; destruct i as [|j]; simpl; [reflexivity | lia].` For the S j case, the goal is an iff but the hypothesis Hi : S j < 1 is absurd, so `lia` closes it. ✓ (lia can close any goal when hypotheses are contradictory).

Now C-related lemmas:
```coq
Lemma C_rho_indep : forall T delta rho1 rho2 t1 t2,
  (forall X, In X (fv_ty T) -> forall x1 x2, rho1 X x1 x2 <-> rho2 X x1 x2) ->
  C T delta rho1 t1 t2 <-> C T delta rho2 t1 t2.

  intros. unfold C. split; intros (v1 & v2 & H1 & H2 & HV); exists v1, v2; repeat split; auto;
  [ apply (proj1 (V_rho_indep T delta rho1 rho2 v1 v2 H)); auto
  | apply (proj2 (V_rho_indep T delta rho1 rho2 v1 v2 H)); auto ].
Qed.
```

`C_value_refl`:

```coq
Lemma multi_trans : forall x y z, x -->* y -> y -->* z -> x -->* z.
Proof.
  intros x y z H. induction H; intros Hz.
  - exact Hz.
  - apply multi_step with y; auto.

Lemma multi_app_l : forall t1 t1' t2,
  t1 -->* t1' -> locally_closed_tm t2 -> tm_app t1 t2 -->* tm_app t1' t2.
Proof.
  intros t1 t1' t2 H. induction H; intros Hlc.
  - apply multi_refl.
  - apply multi_step with (tm_app y t2).
    + apply ST_App1; assumption.
    + apply IHmulti; assumption.
Qed.

Lemma multi_app_r : forall v1 t2 t2',
  value v1 -> t2 -->* t2' -> tm_app v1 t2 -->* tm_app v1 t2'.
Proof.
  intros v1 t2 t2' Hv H. induction H.
  - apply multi_refl.
  - apply multi_step with (tm_app v1 y).
    + apply ST_App2; assumption.
    + apply IHmulti.
Qed.

Lemma multi_tapp : forall t t' U,
  t -->* t' -> locally_closed_ty U -> tm_tapp t U -->* tm_tapp t' U.
Proof.
  intros t t' U H. induction H; intros Hlc.
  - apply multi_refl.
  - apply multi_step with (tm_tapp y U).
    + apply ST_TApp; assumption.
    + apply IHmulti; assumption.
Qed.

Proof. intros; apply multi_step with t'; auto; apply multi_refl. Qed.
```

Lemma ctx_lc_extend : forall Gamma x T,
  (forall y Ty, lookup_context y Gamma = Some Ty -> lc_ty_at 0 Ty) ->
  lc_ty_at 0 T ->
  (forall y Ty, lookup_context y (update Gamma x T) = Some Ty -> lc_ty_at 0 Ty).

Qed.

```coq
Lemma ctx_lc_extend : forall Gamma x T,
  (forall y Ty, lookup_context y Gamma = Some Ty -> lc_ty_at 0 Ty) ->
  lc_ty_at 0 T ->
  (forall y Ty, lookup_context y (update Gamma x T) = Some Ty -> lc_ty_at 0 Ty).
Proof.
  intros Gamma x T Hctx HT y Ty Hy.
  unfold update in Hy. simpl in Hy.
  destruct (Nat.eqb y x) eqn:E.
  - apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst. inversion Hy. subst. exact HT.
  - apply Hctx. exact Hy.
Qed.
```
Wait, `update Gamma x T = (x, T) :: Gamma`; `lookup_context y ((x,T)::Gamma)` = `if Nat.eqb y x then Some T else lookup_context y Gamma`. Let me check the definition of lookup_context: 
```
match Gamma with
| [] => None
| (y, T) :: Gamma' => if Nat.eqb x y then Some T else lookup_context x Gamma'
end
```
It matches on the pair `(y,T)` binding y,T, and compares `Nat.eqb x y` where x is the lookup variable. So for context (x0,T0)::Gamma and looking up y: `if Nat.eqb y x0 then Some T0 else lookup_context y Gamma`. In `ctx_lc_extend`, update Gamma x T = (x,T)::Gamma; looking up y: `if Nat.eqb y x then Some T else lookup_context y Gamma`. ✓. So destruct (Nat.eqb y x), and in the true branch inversion Hy gives Ty = T.

Definition ctx_fv_ty (Gamma : context) : list atom :=
  flat_map (fun p => fv_ty (snd p)) Gamma.
Lemma ctx_fv_ty_lookup : forall Gamma x T, lookup_context x Gamma = Some T -> forall X, In X (fv_ty T) -> In X (ctx_fv_ty Gamma).

Proof: induction Gamma; destruct pair; simpl. Lookup: if eqb x a then Some t else ...; destruct eqb; inversion; etc. Let me prove:
```
Lemma in_ctx_fv_ty : forall Gamma y Ty, lookup_context y Gamma = Some Ty -> forall X, In X (fv_ty Ty) -> In X (ctx_fv_ty Gamma).
Proof.
  induction Gamma as [| [a Ta] Gamma IH]; intros y Ty Hlook X HX; simpl in *.
  - discriminate.
  - destruct (Nat.eqb y a) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E; subst; inversion Hlook; subst. apply in_or_app. left. exact HX.
    + apply in_or_app. right. apply (IH y Ty Hlook X HX).
Qed.
```
Wait, lookup_context y ((a,Ta)::Gamma) = if Nat.eqb y a then Some Ta else lookup y Gamma. ✓. In the true branch, Hlook : Some Ta = Some Ty → Ty = Ta; HX : In X (fv_ty Ty) = In X (fv_ty Ta); goal In X (fv_ty Ta ++ ctx_fv_ty Gamma) → left ✓.

Let me define the goal pieces carefully in the proof script. I'll write:

```
Theorem fundamental : forall Delta Gamma t T,
  has_type Delta Gamma t T ->
  forall (Hctx : forall x Tx, lookup_context x Gamma = Some Tx -> lc_ty_at 0 Tx)
         sigma1 sigma2 delta rho gamma1 gamma2,
    (forall X, lc_ty_at 0 (sigma1 X)) ->
    (forall X, lc_ty_at 0 (sigma2 X)) ->
    (forall x, locally_closed_tm (gamma1 x)) ->
    (forall x, locally_closed_tm (gamma2 x)) ->
    (forall i x1 x2, delta i x1 x2 -> value x1 /\ value x2) ->
    (forall X x1 x2, rho X x1 x2 -> value x1 /\ value x2) ->
    (forall x Tx, lookup_context x Gamma = Some Tx -> C Tx delta rho (gamma1 x) (gamma2 x)) ->
    C T delta rho (close sigma1 gamma1 t) (close sigma2 gamma2 t).
Proof.
  intros Delta Gamma t T H.
  induction H; intros Hctx sigma1 sigma2 delta rho gamma1 gamma2
    Hsig1 Hsig2 Hgam1 Hgam2 Hdelta Hrho Henv.
```

Wait, the induction predicate must include Hctx; since Hctx is not introduced before induction? We do `intros Delta Gamma t T H. induction H; intros Hctx ...`. The goal before induction is `has_type ... -> forall Hctx ..., C ...`? No: `intros Delta Gamma t T H` leaves the goal `forall Hctx sigma1 ... , ... -> C ...` (the remaining binders of the theorem). Then `induction H` generalizes over the goal, which includes the forall Hctx sigma1... binders; the induction predicate is `fun Delta Gamma t T => forall Hctx sigma1..., ... -> C ...`. Good. So each case's IH includes the forall Hctx etc. For T_Abs, the IH (under forall x) is: `forall x, ~In x L -> (forall Hctx sigma1 ..., ... -> C T2 ...)`? Hmm, from the earlier experiments, the IH for a forall-wrapped premise with extra parameters is `forall x, ~In x L -> forall Hctx sigma1..., ...`. The parameters order: the induction predicate's arguments are the extra foralls in the order they appear in the goal, i.e., Hctx sigma1 sigma2 delta rho gamma1 gamma2 then the hypotheses. So H1 : forall x, ~In x L -> forall Hctx sigma1 ..., ... -> C T2 (close σ1 γ1' (open...)) .... ✓.

Hmm, rather than a general lemma, do it inline with the commutation lemmas. It's basically the same work as in has_type_close_lc. Let me write a helper:

```
Lemma close_open_tm : forall sigma gamma gamma' x a t,
  (forall y, locally_closed_tm (gamma y)) ->
  (forall y, locally_closed_tm (gamma' y)) ->
  gamma' x = a ->
  (forall y, In y (fv_tm t) -> gamma' y = gamma y) ->
  close sigma gamma' (open_tm t (tm_fvar x)) = open_tm (close sigma gamma t) a.
Proof.
  intros. unfold close, open_tm.
  rewrite (tm_ty_subst_env_open_tm t sigma 0 (tm_fvar x)).
  simpl.
  rewrite (tm_subst_env_open_tm (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t) gamma' 0 x Hγ').
  rewrite Hx.  (* gamma' x = a *)
  rewrite (tm_subst_env_agree (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t) gamma' gamma).
  - reflexivity.
  - intros y Hy. rewrite fv_tm_ty_subst_env in Hy. apply Hagree; auto.
Qed.
```
Wait, the agreement direction: tm_subst_env_agree (t) gamma1 gamma2 : (forall y, In y (fv_tm t) -> gamma1 y = gamma2 y) -> tm_subst_env gamma1 t = tm_subst_env gamma2 t. So to rewrite gamma'→gamma, use gamma1 := gamma', gamma2 := gamma and the agreement as stated: for y ∈ fv_tm (tm_ty_subst_env σ t) = fv_tm t, γ' y = γ y ✓.

Similarly for T_TAbs:
```
Lemma close_open_tm_ty : forall sigma sigma' gamma x U t,
  (forall X, lc_ty_at 0 (sigma X)) ->
  (forall X, lc_ty_at 0 (sigma' X)) ->
  sigma' x = U ->
  (forall X, In X (fv_tm_ty t) -> sigma' X = sigma X) ->
  (forall y, locally_closed_tm (gamma y)) ->
  close sigma' gamma (open_tm_ty t (Ty_FVar x)) = open_tm_ty (close sigma gamma t) U.
Proof.
  intros. unfold close, open_tm_ty.
  rewrite (tm_ty_subst_env_open_tm_ty t sigma' 0 (Ty_FVar x)).
  simpl. (* reduces ty_subst_env sigma' (FVar x) to sigma' x *)
  rewrite Hx.  (* sigma' x = U *)
  rewrite (tm_ty_subst_env_agree t sigma' sigma).  (* needs agreement on fv_tm_ty t *)
  - rewrite (tm_subst_env_open_tm_ty (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t) gamma 0 U).
    reflexivity.
  - intros X HX. apply Hagree; auto.
```
Hmm, after the first rewrite, the term is `tm_subst_env gamma (open_tm_ty_rec 0 (ty_subst_env sigma' (Ty_FVar x)) (tm_ty_subst_env sigma' t))`. `simpl` reduces ty_subst_env sigma' (Ty_FVar x) to sigma' x. Then rewrite Hx. Then tm_ty_subst_env_agree rewrites tm_ty_subst_env sigma' t to sigma. Then tm_subst_env_open_tm_ty gives tm_subst_env gamma (open_tm_ty_rec 0 U (...)) = open_tm_ty_rec 0 U (tm_subst_env gamma (...)). ✓.

But note the direction of the final equation: `close sigma gamma t = tm_subst_env gamma (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t)`; the RHS goal is `open_tm_ty (close sigma gamma t) U` = open_tm_ty_rec 0 U (tm_subst_env gamma (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t)) ✓ definitionally.

Let me write the proof:

```
Theorem fundamental : forall Delta Gamma t T,
  has_type Delta Gamma t T ->
  forall (Hctx : forall x Tx, lookup_context x Gamma = Some Tx -> lc_ty_at 0 Tx)
         sigma1 sigma2 delta rho gamma1 gamma2,
    (forall X, lc_ty_at 0 (sigma1 X)) ->
    (forall X, lc_ty_at 0 (sigma2 X)) ->
    (forall x, locally_closed_tm (gamma1 x)) ->
    (forall x, locally_closed_tm (gamma2 x)) ->
    (forall i x1 x2, delta i x1 x2 -> value x1 /\ value x2) ->
    (forall X x1 x2, rho X x1 x2 -> value x1 /\ value x2) ->
    (forall x Tx, lookup_context x Gamma = Some Tx -> C Tx delta rho (gamma1 x) (gamma2 x)) ->
    C T delta rho (close sigma1 gamma1 t) (close sigma2 gamma2 t).
Proof.
  intros Delta Gamma t T H.
  induction H; intros Hctx sigma1 sigma2 delta rho gamma1 gamma2
    Hsig1 Hsig2 Hgam1 Hgam2 Hdelta Hrho Henv.
```

```coq
Theorem fundamental : forall Delta Gamma t T,
  has_type Delta Gamma t T ->
  forall (Hctx : forall x Tx, lookup_context x Gamma = Some Tx -> lc_ty_at 0 Tx)
         sigma1 sigma2 delta rho gamma1 gamma2,
    (forall X, lc_ty_at 0 (sigma1 X)) ->
    (forall X, lc_ty_at 0 (sigma2 X)) ->
    (forall x, locally_closed_tm (gamma1 x)) ->
    (forall x, locally_closed_tm (gamma2 x)) ->
    (forall i x1 x2, delta i x1 x2 -> value x1 /\ value x2) ->
    (forall X x1 x2, rho X x1 x2 -> value x1 /\ value x2) ->
    (forall x Tx, lookup_context x Gamma = Some Tx -> C Tx delta rho (gamma1 x) (gamma2 x)) ->
    C T delta rho (close sigma1 gamma1 t) (close sigma2 gamma2 t).
Proof.

  induction H; intros Hctx sigma1 sigma2 delta rho gamma1 gamma2
    Hsig1 Hsig2 Hgam1 Hgam2 Hdelta Hrho Henv.
  - (* T_Var *)

  - (* T_Abs *)
